=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def minCost(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:

        m, n, cost, queue = len(grid), len(grid[0]), 0, deque()
        M, N = range(m), range(n)

        seen = lambda x,y : not x in M or y not in N or not grid[x][y]
        dir = ((),(0,1), (0,-1), (1,0), (-1,0))

        def dfs(x: int,y: int)->None:
            while not seen(x,y):
                (dx,dy), grid[x][y] = dir[grid[x][y]], None
                queue.append((x,y))
                x,y = x+dx, y+dy
            return

        dfs(0, 0)
        cost += 1

        while queue:
            if (m-1, n-1) in queue: return cost
            cost +=1 
            q = len(queue)

            for _ in range(q):
                x, y = queue.popleft()
                for dx,dy in dir[1:]:
                    dfs(x+dx, y+dy)

```python
class Solution:
    def minCost(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:

        m, n, cost, queue = len(grid), len(grid[0]), 0, deque()
        M, N = range(m), range(n)

        seen = lambda x,y : not x in M or y not in N or not grid[x][y]
        dir = ((),(0,1), (0,-1), (1,0), (-1,0))

        def dfs(x: int,y: int)->None:
            while not seen(x,y):
                (dx,dy), grid[x][y] = dir[grid[x][y]], None
                queue.append((x,y))
                x,y = x+dx, y+dy
            return

        dfs(0, 0)
        cost += 1

        while queue:
            if (m-1, n-1) in queue: return cost
            cost +=1 
            q = len(queue)

            for _ in range(q):
                x, y = queue.popleft()
                for dx,dy in dir[1:]:
                    dfs(x+dx, y+dy)
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

```python
class Solution:
    def minCost(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:

        m, n, cost, queue = len(grid), len(grid[0]), 0, deque()
        M, N = range(m), range(n)

        seen = lambda x,y : not x in M or y not in N or not grid[x][y]
        dir = ((),(0,1), (0,-1), (1,0), (-1,0))

        def dfs(x: int,y: int)->None:
            while not seen(x,y):
                (dx,dy), grid[x][y] = dir[grid[x][y]], None
                queue.append((x,y))
                x,y = x+dx, y+dy
            return

        dfs(0, 0)
        cost += 1

        while queue:
            if (m-1, n-1) in queue: return cost
            cost +=1 
            q = len(queue)

            for _ in range(q):
                x, y = queue.popleft()
                for dx,dy in dir[1:]:
                    dfs(x+dx, y+dy)
```

There may be another bug: The algorithm's `dfs` follows zero-cost path from a neighbor, but it doesn't consider that changing the sign of a cell in the zero-cost path could allow reaching other cells at the same cost? Wait, the cost model: You can change signs at any cell, each change costs 1. If you pay cost c to reach a cell, you may also change its sign (cost +1) to go in a different direction. The algorithm processes each cell at cost c and tries all four outgoing directions with cost c+1. That includes changing the sign of that cell. But what about cells along the zero-cost path: when you reach a cell at cost c, you can also change its sign at cost c+1. The algorithm will process each such cell when it is popped from queue at cost c, and try all directions. Good. So all cells in the zero-cost component are in queue at cost c and can be used as decision points. Good.

Potential issue: The algorithm's `dfs` from a neighbor follows arrows without allowing sign changes along the way until the next BFS layer. That's correct because if you pay to enter neighbor, you can then follow its arrow for free; you could also change the sign of intermediate cells, but that would be a new cost and should be considered in next layer. The algorithm will process those intermediate cells in next layer and allow changes. Good.

Potential issue: The algorithm's `dfs` from a neighbor might append cells that are already in the current queue (same cost c+1) but not yet processed. They are marked None, so not appended. But if they are in current queue, they will be processed next layer. Good.

Potential issue: The algorithm's `seen` lambda marks a cell seen as soon as it is appended, not when processed. This is standard for BFS to avoid duplicates. But with 0-1 BFS, marking seen on discovery is okay if all edge weights nonnegative and we process in nondecreasing cost. Since BFS layers by cost, yes. But is it possible to discover a cell at cost c+1 before discovering it at cost c? No, because all cost c cells are processed before cost c+1 cells. However, within processing layer c, a cell might be discovered at cost c+1, and later in the same layer another cell might discover it at cost c+1 (same), no issue. Could it be discovered at cost c later in same layer? No, because cost c cells are already in queue and processed; but wait, a cost c cell could be appended during processing layer c? Let's examine: When processing layer c, we call dfs on neighbors with cost c+1. dfs appends cells at cost c+1. It does not append cost c cells. So no. Initial layer cost 0 appended before loop. So marking seen on discovery is safe.

</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from collections import deque
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        cost = 0
        queue = deque()
        M, N = range(m), range(n)

        seen = lambda x, y: x not in M or y not in N or grid[x][y] is None
        dirs = ((), (0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0))

        def dfs(x: int, y: int) -> None:
            while not seen(x, y):
                dx, dy = dirs[grid[x][y]]
                grid[x][y] = None
                queue.append((x, y))
                x += dx
                y += dy

        dfs(0, 0)

        while queue:
            if (m - 1, n - 1) in queue:
                return cost

            cost += 1
            q = len(queue)

            for _ in range(q):
                x, y = queue.popleft()
                for dx, dy in dirs[1:]:
                    dfs(x + dx, y + dy)

        return cost
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from collections import deque
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        cost = 0
        queue = deque()
        M, N = range(m), range(n)

        seen = lambda x, y: x not in M or y not in N or grid[x][y] is None
        dirs = ((), (0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0))

        def dfs(x: int, y: int) -> None:
            while not seen(x, y):
                dx, dy = dirs[grid[x][y]]
                grid[x][y] = None
                queue.append((x, y))
                x += dx
                y += dy

        dfs(0, 0)

        while queue:
            if (m - 1, n - 1) in queue:
                return cost

            cost += 1
            q = len(queue)

            for _ in range(q):
                x, y = queue.popleft()
                for dx, dy in dirs[1:]:
                    dfs(x + dx, y + dy)

        return cost
